OUTPUT: set turn="review", fill the "review" object, and set every other section to null.
REVIEW TURN. Independently audit EVERY candidate in `candidates`; each is a
hypothesis from the discovery turn, not a trusted conclusion. Return exactly one
decision per candidate_id.

Return obligation_class (execution_compatibility per D4, target_coverage per D5),
scope_basis, trigger_basis and the five checks of J1, each with status
established/refuted/unknown, a specific reason and exact evidence:
scope_contract (J1a, D2); feasible_trigger (J1b): for execution compatibility a
finite actual target execution, for target coverage the changed code together
with the build, setup or input framing that would execute it and that the target
cannot produce; observable_consequence (J1c);
alternatives_ruled_out (J1d, D8), including counterevidence supplied with the
candidate; identity_preserved (J1e; for timing also T1).

Use effect blocked_target_coverage only for target_coverage. For target_coverage
fill every coverage_witness field; for the other class use empty strings. These
fields do not replace the citations of each check.

decision=keep requires all five checks established with source and harness
evidence, a finite_witness and no decisive missing fact. decision=dismiss requires
a grounded refutation or equivalent route (J6). Otherwise return uncertain and
request the missing facts (E3). For maintenance, unresolved timing does not
invalidate a currently established obligation (J2).

Then audit the whole scope again (J4): assess every selected change ID and the
four foundations with evidence, challenge both dismissals (J6) and retained
gaps (J1). Out-of-scope items (D7) are not obligations. New evidence contains
contracts retrieved for earlier requests (`evidence`); `citation_errors` lists earlier
citations the program could not verify; cite valid locations instead. Request
exact symbols or paths only for decisive missing facts (E3).
If the evidence establishes a distinct obligation that is NOT among `candidates`
(for example one that only became visible with the new evidence), report it in
`new_candidates` with the same fields as a discovery blocker and mark its change
group as blocked in the scope audit. It is reviewed in a separate turn; do not
add a decision for it here.

Destroying a UCL tree is a graph traversal: releasing a container releases\n223| * its children, which may be containers themselves. Doing that on the C stack\n224| * makes cleanup depth proportional to the nesting depth of the document, so a\n225| * deeply nested (and possibly attacker supplied) input could exhaust the stack\n226| * while being freed - long after the parser had returned successfully.\n227| *\n228| * So the traversal is explicit instead. An object only reaches the worklist\n229| * once its last reference is gone, which means it is already detached from the\n230| * tree and nothing else can observe it; its `next` pointer is therefore free\n231| * for us to reuse as the worklist link. That keeps destruction allocation free\n232| * and its stack usage constant.\n233| */\n234|struct ucl_dtor_worklist {\n235|\tucl_object_t *head;\n236|};\n237|\n238|static inline void\n239|ucl_dtor_worklist_push(struct ucl_dtor_worklist *wl, ucl_object_t *obj)\n240|{\n241|\tobj->next = wl->head;\n242|\twl->head = obj;\n243|}\n244|\n245|static inline ucl_object_t *\n246|ucl_dtor_worklist_pop(struct ucl_dtor_worklist *wl)\n247|{\n248|\tucl_object_t *obj = wl->head;\n249|\n250|\tif (obj != NULL) {\n251|\t\twl->head = obj->next;\n252|\t\tobj->next = NULL;\n253|\t}\n254|\n255|\treturn obj;\n256|}\n257|\n258|/*\n259| * Drop one reference to `obj` and queue it for destruction if that was the\n260| * last one. `obj` must already be unlinked from whatever contained it.\n261| */\n262|static void\n263|ucl_dtor_worklist_unref(void *ptr, void *ud)\n264|{\n265|\tucl_object_t *obj = (ucl_object_t *) ptr;\n266|\tstruct ucl_dtor_worklist *wl = (struct ucl_dtor_worklist *) ud;\n267|\n268|\tif (obj == NULL) {\n269|\t\treturn;\n270|\t}\n271|\n272|#ifdef HAVE_ATOMIC_BUILTINS\n273|\tif (__sync_sub_and_fetch(&obj->ref, 1) == 0) {\n274|#else\n275|\tif (--obj->ref == 0) {\n276|#endif\n277|\t\tucl_dtor_worklist_push(wl, obj);\n278|\t}\n279|}\n280|\n281|/*\n282| * Queue every element of an implicit-array chain starting at `obj`. The chain\n283| * links are read before each element is unreffed, since unreffing may reuse\n284| * them for the worklist.\n285| */\n286|static inline void\n287|ucl_dtor_worklist_unref_chain(struct ucl_dtor_worklist *wl, ucl_object_t *obj)\n288|{\n289|\tucl_object_t *tmp;\n290|\n291|\twhile (obj != NULL) {\n292|\t\ttmp = obj->next;\n293|\t\tucl_dtor_worklist_unref(obj, wl);\n294|\t\tobj = tmp;\n295|\t}\n296|}\n297|\n298|/*\n299| * Release a whole subtree. `obj` must have already reached zero references;\n300| * with `allow_rec` its implicit-array siblings are released as well.\n301| */\n302|static void\n303|ucl_object_free_internal(ucl_object_t *obj, bool allow_rec)\n304|{\n305|\tstruct ucl_dtor_worklist wl = {NULL};\n306|\tucl_object_t *cur, *sub, *tmp;\n307|\n308|\tif (obj == NULL) {\n309|\t\treturn;\n310|\t}\n311|\n312|\t/*\n313|\t * The head is dead already, so it goes straight onto the worklist;
